ParlaMint-SI_2011-03-02-SDZ5-Redna-25.ana.u224	Dear President, the well-respected others! Thank you very much. Thank you very much. The purpose of the law on the restriction of shopping bags is already described in the title. Our aim, therefore, is to achieve effective legislation that will in a meaningful way encourage, in particular, those who offer their customers, free of charge, shopping bags as carrier bags to introduce a different policy. The shopping bags represent a packaging that is essential and useful. However, the use of this packaging has changed in recent years. If it was sometimes considered important when a person bought it or obtained it at the store, brought it home, laid it down and stored it for the next time, it is now considered that every time you get to the cashier's office, whether it be a grocery store, whether it be a textile store, whether it be a shoe store or any trade, they will offer you a free bag, which often does not end where you should, so on, in waste or in separate collection of plastic waste. The weight bags represent a very small percentage of the waste from the packaging, but on the surface they represent a very large percentage. It is because of their ease that the bags are very mobile and quickly they can easily pollute the environment. In the Pacific, there is a hundreds of kilometres of global flows that are surrounded by those bags, and among these are probably the bags that we have thrown into the environment in Slovenia. Our aim is to introduce an environmental contribution that will encourage the trader to no longer offer the bags free of charge, and that any bag, whether plastic, paper or any other, will also encourage the buyer, the final user of this package, to bring the bag with him as it used to be, and thus to store, value and use it several times. This will avoid unnecessary environmental burdens and avoid unnecessary continuous, continuous production of new packaging, which, of course, uses important resources, important production of resources and important energy. We would like to see the bag you get in the store again as an exception and no longer a rule. At the initiative of environmentalists without borders, i.e. those who have organised the "Clean Slovenia" action, we hope that, in this first reading, we will be able to clear up some of the dilemmas so that we can find out what is yet to be improved in this law, we ourselves believe that amendments to this law should be made to determine which exceptions they are, of course, we cannot expect to place an environmental burden on the bags to which you are giving fruit or vegetables, and that we will maintain the provision that the collected money is allocated to natural persons for environmental investment through the Eco Fund. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-03-02-SDZ5-Redna-25.ana.u226	Thank you very much, Mr Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! Let me first say that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia welcomes the proposaler's initiative and the purpose, I stress the purpose of supporting the bill. However, it also considers that systemic solutions should be sought within the framework of existing sub-legislative acts which cover the entire area of packaging and packaging waste, as part of the amendments and complements to the environmental taxes regulation, which is in preparation and will be transmitted to the public by the end of March, and through the packaging and packaging regulation. However, the public treatment of the municipal waste management regulation has already been completed and will contribute significantly to efficient separate collection of waste packagings. With regard to this position, let me point out some key points relevant to understanding this issue. We have written them down more precisely in the opinion that we forwarded to the National Assembly in February this year. European Directive 94/62 of 20 December 1994 on packaging and packaging waste applies to all packaging placed on the market in the Community and to all packaging used in industrial, commercial, office, commercial, commercial, service, household or any other environment resulting from it, irrespective of the material used. So here are the bags. The government itself gives great importance to the principle of 'polluter pays', which is among the fundamental principles of environmental policy and legislation in Slovenia. This is also evident from the comprehensive system of environmental duties introduced under the Environmental Protection Act, which promotes more sustainable patterns of production and consumption. In the price of each bag corresponding to the definition of packaging, all carrier and separator bags are already included in the so-called packaging and environmental duty. The packaging shall be paid to the packaging company by the person who supplies the packaging, in this case the bag, for the handling of the packaging waste, in order to ensure the handling of the packaging, in this case with the waste bag. The environmental duty is the amount paid by the same person when he places the bag on the market and is the income of the Republic of Slovenia’s integrated budget. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia therefore notes that there would be duplication of duties by introducing a new environmental contribution, which is unacceptable. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia further notes that Article 8 of the Law defines the intentional use of funds obtained through an environmental contribution, but the intentional use of the funds collected is not in accordance with national public finance legislation, as it is contrary to the adopted concept of an integrated budget. Under the Directive, economic instruments may only be used to promote the achievement of the objectives of the Directive, without the purpose of Article 8, paragraph 1, of the Law, which could constitute a violation of Directive 94/62. The proposal therefore infringes on the content regulated by existing EU legislation, in accordance with harmonised, legal acts. In view of the purpose of the proposal for a law to restrict the use of shopping bags, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia considers that it is necessary to verify that this intention or objectives, in accordance with Directive 94/62, can be achieved already by amending and supplementing the existing regulations. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia therefore considers that the issue of shopping bags should be addressed more comprehensively and within the framework of the measures established under the Environmental Protection Act, the Environmental Taxation Regulations, packaging and waste packaging regulations and the Municipal Waste Management Regulation and, accordingly, to promote efforts aimed at raising public awareness of packaging and waste packaging management, to prevent waste packaging from being produced, to reuse and recovery, to favour recycling and to improve the separate collection of waste packaging. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-03-02-SDZ5-Redna-25.ana.u228	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Good-bye to all of you once again. These numbers are worrisome, even if we look at where everything is and how much in nature we can see hanging or lying unscrupulously discarded old bags. Unfortunately, we are still far from that, even though things are improving. Let me touch the law itself. In the DeSUS Group, we agree with the warnings of the competent ministry that the purpose of the law is good, but that it is limping in the implementation provision itself. The draft law refers to the Directive of the European Parliament of 1994 on packaging and packaging waste, applicable to all packaging and all packaging waste and articles designed and intended for filling at the place of sale. This is how paper bags and plastic bags are considered as packaging. As set out in the Directive, not all bags are packaging. As an example, I can mention garbage bags, food bags in the household and so on. However, paper and plastic carrier and separation bags shall be considered as packaging. The rules on handling them are governed by the Regulation on the management of packaging and packaging waste, which has also transposed the Directive into our legal order. The draft law itself also introduces an environmental contribution that is charged at the time of sale or free of charge for the provision of shopping bags for the storage and personal transfer of goods, and the manner of accounting for the environmental contribution is determined by the Minister responsible for the finances in accordance with this proposal. So the body implementing this law is the Ministry of Finance. and not the Ministry of Environment and Space. We also agree with the Government's view that a more detailed provision should be made for Article 8 which regulates the purposeful use of the funds raised. The method of application as set out in the proposal is not in accordance with Article 15 of that Directive, which provides that Member States shall adopt economic instruments to promote the achievement of the objective of this Directive. The possibility of earmarking funds is also not in line with national public finance legislation, as it is contrary to the concept of an integrated budget. In addition, under this Directive we should notify the European Commission that we are preparing a legal act relating to packaging. This is what we have to do before we adopt such an act. A number of provisions should be defined in more detail in the draft law in order not to conflict with the Directive. I will not go into detail because our concerns are similar to those of the government. In the DeSUS Group, we will decide in the course of the debate whether or not we will support the proposal for a law. If the Government convinces us that a more careful amendment to the sub-legislative acts covering the entire packaging area would be waste packaging, then we will not support the bill. If the government does not make convincing arguments, we will support the bill because we believe that any law with good amendments can also be corrected. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-03-02-SDZ5-Redna-25.ana.u238	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. Dear representatives of the Ministry, colleagues and colleagues! In Mrs Zares, we support the proposal for a law limiting the use of shopping bags, which we have also proposed in the legislative process in cooperation with Members and Members of coalition groups. The bill introduces an environmental contribution that is charged at each sale of a shopping bag and aims to significantly reduce the demand for bags and encourage the use of multi-use bags and other environmentally harmful types of packaging. At this point, it should be stressed that the aim of the law is not to raise funds with an environmental tax, but that due to the corresponding high environmental contribution, plastic bags will no longer be used, so that, ideally, the revenue from the environmental contribution budget will be null and void. Unlike neighbouring Italy, we have not decided on a total ban on the use of plastic shopping bags, which of all shopping bags are also the most burdensome for the environment. A complete ban on our conduct is not possible due to the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on packaging and packaging waste, which provides in Article 19 that Member States do not impede the placing on the market of packaging which complies with the provisions of this Directive on their territory. The same directive allows for the burden of environmental contribution and therefore the law applies to this mechanism. We have also prepared the draft law in cooperation with the NGO Ecology Without Borders. So let me also remind you of their arguments in support of the bill. The call for support for the proposal for a law restricting the use of plastic bags stated that Members and Members will help us to take the first steps towards a more sustainable management of limited natural resources through the expression of support and the adoption of the proposed law. The law will help us to move from recycling to preventing waste generation, which is also the first in the waste hierarchy under the European Union guidelines. Every year we spend between 4,000 and 5,000 billion plastic bags in the world, of which between 300 and 600 million in Slovenia. This means at least 150 bags per person per year. On average, discard each bag after 20 minutes of use. This is also why the annual production of plastic bags in Slovenia consumes as much as 5 million litres of oil. Among the key arguments why plastic shopping bags are problematic and harmful to the environment, however, we point out that plastic bags are estimated to take up to a thousand years to completely decompose. In the process of decommissioning, however, there are increasingly small particles that bind themselves to poisons and contaminate the earth, water, and air. In this way, they also enter the human body through the food chain. A billion animals die each year from plastic feeding, usually replaced by food and food, and many in plastic bags become entangled and die. Bags in the sea, rivers, tree branches and roads are disruptive to the surrounding area and spoil the image of our country. The chemical components used in the manufacturing process are proven irreversibly polluting the environment. Only collecting, transporting and removing plastic bags creates additional environmental burdens. In the final stage, we are consumers who also cover the costs of so-called free plastic bags in the form of higher product prices when buying products. An example of good practice in restricting the use of plastic bags is Ireland, where already in 2002 they introduced a special tax on plastic bags, which traders shared free of charge in stores. After the adoption of the law, 0.33 euros had to be paid for the purchase of one bag. At the time of the introduction of the fee, an intensive promotion campaign against the use of plastic bags took place. The result of the law was excellent, as the share of plastic bags fell by 94% within a few weeks. Despite initial opposition from traders, during the years of application of the law, they have all become major advocates and promoters of the non-use of plastic shopping bags. Ireland is also today a much cleaner country because of its reduced use of plastic bags. The revenue from the tax on plastic bags in Ireland is transferred to a special fund called the Environment Fund, which is intended to support the implementation of projects in the field of environmental protection. And according to Ireland's example, a proposal for a law restricting the use of plastic bags is also being prepared today. At this point, it should also be noted that before we decided to introduce the law, the competent Ministry of Environment and Space proposed that the environmental pollution tax regulation should be amended and supplemented accordingly by introducing corresponding higher levies which would have a restrictive effect on the production and use of packaging. In that proposal, we advocated a comprehensive restriction on the use of packaging and not just plastic shopping bags. This would contribute to reducing the amount of waste produced by obvious positive effects. We are convinced that one of the reasons for the inefficiency of the current state of packaging taxation is on the basis of the values set out in the Environmental Regulation for environmental pollution due to the generation of waste packaging. The current taxation is only detectable for the packaging made of PVC, while for everything else the packaging is only symbolic. The country collects 400,000 euros a year from these other packages. As an example, it is possible to specify free shopping bags. According to their average weight, they are now taxed only with 4.5 million euros, says 0.0000045 euros. So practically nothing. In other words, it is necessary to collect as many as 217 000,865 bags for 1 euro. Therefore, we have proposed that the tax on such packaging should be raised in a way that would encourage more rational and environmentally friendly treatment of packaging. Since, despite repeated initiatives and proposals at the Ministry of Environment and Space, we have not responded to our initiative, Members of the Zares Group and SD have tabled a proposal for a law signed by more than 30 Members and Members of Parliament. And it is only after that that we have raised an appropriate interest in our proposals at the Ministry and, earlier this year, a very concrete agreement and a guarantee that, before the adoption of the law, they will make appropriate amendments to the two regulations to replace the proposed solutions to the law that we are considering today. At the meeting with Minister Žarnic, we agreed with the Minister that the use of shopping bags should be limited and that there are more ways to do so : either by a new law or by amending the two regulations governing this area. At that time, we also agreed to continue the drafting of the bill, which will also provide support to the Ministry of Environment and Space and the Ministry of Finance. At the Ministry of Environment and Space, however, they also committed themselves to preparing an appropriate timetable and the content of amendments to the two key regulations in this area. In particular, due to the fact that, today, when we consider the proposal for a law at first reading, the relevant regulations have not yet been adopted which would adequately replace the law, are not yet ready and are not presented to the public, nor are their drafts presented, because of these facts in the Zares Group, we believe that the new policy should be continued and complemented by the adoption of the law in the context of the second reading and taking into account the comments and proposals of the interested public. If and when the Minister for the Environment has announced that the Ministry will adopt appropriate regulations before the adoption of the law, allowing it to limit the use of plastic shopping bags more quickly, then only then will we in the Zares Group – a new policy decide whether it makes sense by voting against the law to enable the withdrawal of the law from the adoption process. Today, the National Assembly does not yet have these assurances, so we propose that we send the bill to a second reading and thus strengthen the necessary public debate on the issue of shopping bags, as well as a broader debate on waste management and municipal waste in its entirety. We must recognise that, in the event of the adoption of regulations at the competent ministry, this necessary public debate would not have been or would have been held in another context, as we did last year, for example, in the approximately period when, due to the ineffective implementation of waste management policies at the request of the Court of Auditors and the proposal by the mandate-holder, we discussed the replacement of the former Minister for Environment and Space, Karl Erjavac. Erjavec, let me remind you, at that time, he freely ordered famous manners from the waste management issue when he wondered whether he was really responsible for every trash can in Slovenia. It is time for Slovenia to also start by preventing the generation of unnecessary waste. This is also one of the key messages of the law. For this reason, we will support the proposal in the Really Group – a new policy to conclude that the law on restricting the use of shopping bags is appropriate for further use.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-03-02-SDZ5-Redna-25.ana.u240	Ladies and gentlemen! The first thing that stabbed us in the eye is the fact that the environmental contribution is paid for all bags, regardless of the material from which they are made, even for bags made of textile or paper. The difference is only at the level of the environmental contribution. This is already fundamentally contrary to the purpose of the law, which is intended to promote the use of multi-use bags, which are clearly more durable bags of textiles. The next thing that disturbed us is the fact that, by implementing such a law, there can be a marked duplication of duties on packaging, including all carrier and separate bags. At present, traders and all other operators that, for example, place the bag on the market are already paying packaging and environmental pollution charges to the first waste management company, the second to the state budget. These charges shall provide funds for the management of waste packaging. It is worth mentioning, however, that the level of packaging is currently set at EUR 112 per tonne or 11 cents per kilogram, while the estimated environmental contribution per bag would be between EUR 20 and 50 cents. This would mean an additional financial burden on consumers, which even now, despite the fact that packaging and environmental duties are the costs of the trader and are not shown in the invoice, these two charges are paid because traders include their price in the price of the products for which these bags are intended. These are so-called separation bags. By the way, the law also does not define the separate and bearer bags, which brings a lot of uncertainty, since we do not know whether the environmental contribution relates to all bags, including those used in the purchase of fruit and vegetables, or only to those delivered to the consumer at the register. This could lead to people starting to use free separation bags instead of carry bags, which would not reduce the use of plastic bags, as this would encourage traders to give even more aggressive delivery of free separation bags, which will start with already overloaded cashiers, to whom the proposed law already places the greatest burden of difficult communication with customers. The deliberate use of funds derived from environmental contributions under this Act is also a questionable solution, as it is very questionable how the funds obtained could be diverted to the irreparable financial incentives or costs set out in this Act. Such an arrangement also opposes the concept of an integrated budget, which provides that all budgetary revenue is spent on all budgetary expenditure, which prevents some direct budgetary consumers from being entitled to the budgetary appropriations already reserved. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, we believe that it is necessary to carefully consider whether we want to continue the process of adopting this law, because in our view the negative effects resulting from the enforcement of the law would outweigh the positive effects. This does not mean, however, that the issue of packaging reduction and recycling does not need to be addressed. We are all aware that packages for single use, whether they are plastic bags, plastic bottles or other packaging, are simply too much. Measures in the field of waste packaging and other municipal waste should be strengthened, in particular by the accelerated establishment of regional waste separation centres, while measures should be taken to plan the reduction of the use of packaging. First of all, the proportion of municipal waste processed from 24 % should be increased to at least 50 % and the quantity of permanently decommissioned waste should be significantly reduced and the use of processed substances should be indirectly reduced to the use of natural resources. All this can be done, according to the Members of the Slovenian People's Party, through the consistent implementation of existing legislation or its implementation, in particular the Regulation on the management of packaging and waste packaging and the Regulation on Environmental Taxation for Environmental Pollution as a result of the formation of waste packaging. In our view, the area of packaging handling and its restriction must be resolved at a systemic level, taking into account all the positive and negative effects both for the economy and for the environment and for people's lives. Therefore, in the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we call on the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to prepare an analysis of the use of packaging and the processing of packaging waste, including plastic bags, and to include solutions in the sub-laws themselves that will follow the purpose and objectives of the proposed law. In our opinion, it is only possible to ensure an adequate solution to the problem, excessive and re-use of packaging, as these measures must be aimed at reducing all types of packaging, not just plastic bags, which represent only a few percent of all waste packaging. Members of the Slovenian People's Party do not support the proposed law, at least in such a form, for all the above and reasoned reasons. Thank you.
